{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}_{\alpha}{\bar{y}}
is true in
M
if, and only if, there is
an injective function
f\colon M_{\alpha}\to M
such that
(fs)({\bar{y}})=s({\bar{x}})
for all
s\in M_{\alpha}
.
Now choose any bijection
g\colon M\to M
that extends
f^{-1}
, i.e.
(g\circ f)(s)=s
for

s\in M_{\alpha}
. The Skolem extension
M[{\bar{x}}^{\prime}\mapsto F]
where
F\colon M\rightarrow A^{k}
extends
any
t\in M
to
t[{\bar{x}}^{\prime}\mapsto(gt)({\bar{x}})]
clearly satisfies
{\bar{x}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

(because

is a bijection).
Further, if an assignment
t[{\bar{x}}^{\prime}\mapsto(gt)({\bar{x}})]
satisfies
\alpha({\bar{x}}^{\prime})
then the
assignment
gt
satisfies
\alpha({\bar{x}})
; hence
gt\in M_{\alpha}
which means that

t({\bar{y}})=(gt)({\bar{x}})
, and thus implies that
t[{\bar{x}}^{\prime}\mapsto(gt)({\bar{x}})]
satisfies
{\bar{x}}^{\prime}={\bar{y}}
.
For the converse, assume that for some tuple of values
{\bar{c}}
such that
\alpha({\bar{c}})
holds,
there are more assignments
s\in M
with
s({\bar{x}})={\bar{c}}
than assignments
t
with
t({\bar{y}})={\bar{c}}
.
Then any Skolem extension
M[{\bar{x}}^{\prime}\mapsto F]
that satisfies
{\bar{x}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

, and hence also

{\bar{x}}\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

must have more assignments

, so the formula on the right hand side does not hold for this multiteam.
∎

Somewhat deeper arguments are needed to show that multiteam independence logic
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\mathbin{\perp\!\!\!\perp}]
is powerful enough to express all of
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

.
The proof is based on ideas from
(

)
.
A similar result for probabilistic team semantics was established in
(

)
.

Proposition 3.

{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}]\precneqq{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\mathbin{\perp\!\!\!\perp}]
.
Especially,
{\bar{x}}\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{\bar{y}}
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\varphi_{\mathbin{\hbox to8.83pt{\vbox to6.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\enskip\lower-3.24828pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-5.01947pt}{-1.37326pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}({\bar{x}},{\bar{y}})\mathbin{:=}\forall{\bar{z}}\forall a\forall b\big(

\displaystyle({\bar{z}}\neq{\bar{x}}\land{\bar{z}}\neq{\bar{y}})\lor({\bar{z}}\neq{\bar{y}}\land a\neq b)\lor

\displaystyle({\bar{z}}={\bar{y}}\land a=b)\lor(({\bar{z}}={\bar{y}}\lor a=b)\land{\bar{z}}\mathbin{\perp\!\!\!\perp}ab)\big).

Let us explain the formula
\varphi_{\mathbin{\hbox to8.83pt{\vbox to6.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\enskip\lower-3.24828pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-5.01947pt}{-1.37326pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}=\forall{\bar{z}},a,b(\varphi_{1}\lor\varphi_{2}\lor\varphi_{3}\lor\varphi_{4})

on an intuitive level before we formally verify that it actually states what is claimed here.
The variable tuple
{\bar{z}}
is used as a bridge between the values of
{\bar{x}}
and
{\bar{y}}
,
and we are not interested in values of
{\bar{z}}
that do not coincide with either
of both and remove these using the first disjunct
\varphi_{1}
.
The actual check, whether all values for
{\bar{x}}
and
{\bar{y}}
occur equally often happens
in the final subformula
\varphi_{4}
, but we have to prepare the values we want to pass
to it.
The reasoning behind the first-order part of
\varphi_{4}
(that it only accepts
assignments in which
a
and
b
agree, or if
{\bar{z}}
assumes the same value as
{\bar{y}}

does) is that we have to verify that every value of
{\bar{x}}
occurs at least as often
for
{\bar{y}}
, hence, whenever
{\bar{z}}
equals
{\bar{y}}
we are at a “good” value and
thus put no restriction on
a
and
b
.
But if we see an assignment in which
{\bar{z}}
agrees with
{\bar{x}}
we enforce that
a
and
b

coincide.
Accordingly, if a value
{\bar{u}}
occurs more often for
{\bar{x}}
than for
{\bar{y}}
it must
be the case that the probability that
{\bar{z}}
assumes
{\bar{u}}
is larger if one knows
that in fact
a
and
b
agree, which violates the independence atom
{\bar{z}}\mathbin{\perp\!\!\!\perp}ab
.
The subformula
\varphi_{2}
simply helps us to eliminate the assignments that are
forbidden in
\varphi_{4}
, while
\varphi_{3}
helps us to get rid of those assignments
in which
{\bar{z}}={\bar{y}}\neq{\bar{x}}
and
a=b
holds.
This is necessary because every assignment satisfying
{\bar{z}}={\bar{x}}\neq{\bar{y}}
must be
distributed to
\varphi_{4}
wherefore we already have assignments in which
a
and

b
agree.
But the assignments in which
{\bar{z}}
assumes the same value, but this time agrees
with
{\bar{y}}
are necessary to “counter” these and hence equally often must
map
a
and
b
to different values.
The subformula
\varphi_{3}
takes its role by accepting such assignments where
a=b
.
Notice that in the formula given by Galliani for team semantics a similar formula like
\varphi_{3}
is not necessary because it only matters that for every
value that
{\bar{z}}
might assume for all combinations of values for
a
and
b
there
are assignments that actually take these values, but their multiplicity does not count.

Proof of Proposition
3
.

Assume
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}{\bar{x}}\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{\bar{y}}
and let
M^{\prime}
be the multiteam after assigning all values
to the variables
{\bar{z}},a
and
b
, i.e.
M^{\prime}=M[{\bar{z}}\mapsto A^{|{\bar{z}}|}][a\mapsto A][b\mapsto A]
.
To prove that indeed
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M^{\prime}}(\varphi_{1}\lor\varphi_{2}\lor\varphi_{3}\lor\varphi_{4})
let

M_{1}:=M^{\prime}_{\varphi_{1}}
and
M_{2}:=M^{\prime}_{\varphi_{2}}
.
Recall that
M_{\varphi}
is the submultiset of all assignments in
M
that satisfy
\varphi
.
Therefore, all remaining assignments
s\in M^{\prime\prime}:=M^{\prime}\setminus(M_{1}\uplus M_{2})

either satisfy
{\bar{z}}={\bar{y}}
or
{\bar{z}}={\bar{x}}\neq{\bar{y}}\land a=b
.
Assignments of the first kind can be divided further based on whether they
satisfy
{\bar{x}}={\bar{y}}
: put all assignments of
M^{\prime\prime}
that do not obey this equation
and moreover agree on
a
and
b
into
M_{3}
, i.e.
M_{3}:=M^{\prime\prime}_{{\bar{x}}\neq{\bar{y}}\land a=b}
,
and let
M_{4}:=M^{\prime\prime}\setminus M_{3}
.
It follows immediately from the definitions of
M_{i}
that

\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M_{i}}\varphi_{i}
for
i=1,2,3
.
What is left to show is that indeed
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M_{4}}\varphi_{4}
holds.
The first-order part, that is
{\bar{z}}={\bar{y}}\lor a=b
, of
\varphi_{4}
is satisfied by

M_{4}
since every assignments
s\in M^{\prime\prime}
satisfies it and
M_{4}\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}M^{\prime\prime}
.
We claim that for all values which
{\bar{z}}
may assume in
M_{4}
every value combination
for
a
and

occurs equally often in
M_{4}
.

4.

For all
{\bar{c}}\in T(M_{4})({\bar{z}})
and
d,e,f,g\in A
with

\hat{M}:=(M_{4})_{{\bar{z}}={\bar{c}}}
we have
|\hat{M}_{ab=de}|=|\hat{M}_{ab=fg}|
.

1.

Split the assignments in
\hat{M}
according to whether they satisfy
{\bar{x}}={\bar{y}}

into
\hat{M}_{{\bar{x}}={\bar{y}}}
and
\hat{M}_{{\bar{x}}\neq{\bar{y}}}
.
By construction of
M_{4}
we conclude that
\hat{M}_{{\bar{x}}={\bar{y}}}
can be written as

\tilde{M}[a\mapsto A][b\mapsto A]
for some
\tilde{M}
, since we have
distributed every
s\in M^{\prime\prime}
with
s({\bar{x}})=s({\bar{y}})
into
M_{4}
.
Regarding
\hat{M}_{{\bar{x}}\neq{\bar{y}}}
, we observe that the number of assignments
mapping
{\bar{x}}
to
{\bar{c}}
is the same as the number of those mapping
{\bar{y}}
to
{\bar{c}}

because by assumption we know that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}{\bar{x}}\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}

holds.
The assignments of the first kind must map
a
and
b
to identical values,
while the assignments of the second kind must map
a
and
b
to different
values.
But which values those variables take is in no other way restricted and hence
they occur equally often for all possibilities
d,e,f
and
g
.

From this claim it immediately follows that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M_{4}}{\bar{z}}\mathbin{\perp\!\!\!\perp}ab
,
which concludes the proof of the left-to-right direction.

For the converse direction let
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\varphi_{\mathbin{\hbox to8.83pt{\vbox to6.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\enskip\lower-3.24828pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-5.01947pt}{-1.37326pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{

{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}
and let
M^{\prime}
be as above.
That means
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M^{\prime}}(\varphi_{1}\lor\varphi_{2}\lor\varphi_{3}\lor\varphi_{4})
holds
and hence
M^{\prime}=M_{1}\uplus M_{2}\uplus M_{3}\uplus M_{4}
such that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M_{i}}\varphi_{i}

for
i=1,2,3,4
.
Towards a contradiction, assume that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\nvDash}_{M}{\bar{x}}\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

, thus a value

{\bar{c}}\in T(M)({\bar{x}})
exists such that
|M_{{\bar{x}}={\bar{c}}}|>|M_{{\bar{y}}={\bar{c}}}|
.
First, notice that all assignments in
M^{\prime}
which satisfy
{\bar{z}}={\bar{x}}\neq{\bar{y}}\land a=b

must belong to
M_{4}
as they falsify all other formulae
\varphi_{i}
for

.
Consider the submultiteam
\hat{M}
of

that consists of all assignments
s

satisfying
s({\bar{z}})={\bar{c}}
.
We can divide
\hat{M}
into the two multiteams
\hat{M}_{{\bar{z}}={\bar{y}}}
and
\hat{M}_{{\bar{z}}\neq{\bar{y}}}
.
Because
|M_{{\bar{x}}={\bar{c}}}|>|M_{{\bar{y}}={\bar{c}}}|
it follows that also
|\hat{M}_{{\bar{z}}\neq{\bar{y}}}|>|\hat{M}_{{\bar{z}}={\bar{y}}}|
.
But all assignments in
\hat{M}_{{\bar{z}}\neq{\bar{y}}}
must agree on
a
and
b
, as
otherwise the first-order part of
\varphi_{4}
would not be satisfied by
M_{4}
.
At this point we may safely assume that there are two different values
d\neq e
in
A
,
as otherwise all multiteams with base
A
must satisfy the inclusion
{\bar{x}}\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{\bar{y}}
.
Accordingly, in
\hat{M}
, the probability that
{\bar{z}}={\bar{c}}
holds is larger given
the fact that
a=b=d
than if one knows
a=d\neq e=b
, i.e.
\pr_{\hat{M}}({\bar{z}}={\bar{c}}\mid ab=dd)>\pr_{\hat{M}}({\bar{z}}={\bar{c}}\mid ab=de)
.
This projects back to
M_{4}
, because
\hat{M}
is just the collection of all
assignments
s
in
M_{4}
with
s({\bar{z}})={\bar{c}}
.
Thus,
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\nvDash}_{M_{4}}\varphi_{4}
and hence
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\nvDash}_{M}\varphi_{\mathbin{\hbox to8.83pt{\vbox to6.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\enskip\lower-3.24828pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.125pt}{-1.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-5.01947pt}{-1.37326pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}({\bar{x}},{\bar{y}})
.

The inclusion is proper because
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}]
is union closed, while
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\mathbin{\perp\!\!\!\perp}]
is not.
∎

However, it is by no means the case that all connections between logics with team semantics carry over to the
setting of multiteam semantics.
A very interesting issue is the relationship between independence logic on one side,
and inclusion-exclusion logic on the other side. The fact that
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{T}}}[\subseteq,{}\mathbin{\operatorname{|}}{}]
has the full expressive power
of independence logic
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{T}}}[\perp]
and, in fact of any logic
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{T}}}[\Omega]
based on atomic dependencies
that are first-order (or even existential second-order) definable
(
Galliani12
)
has been of fundamental importance for
many results in team semantics. We show that analogous statements for multiteam semantics fail, in a rather strong sense,
and this applies not just to inclusion and exclusion, but to any combination of downwards closed and union closed atomic dependencies.

Theorem 5.

Let
{\bar{\alpha}}
be any collection of downwards closed atoms and
{\bar{\beta}}
be any collection of union closed atoms.
There is no formula
\psi\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[{\bar{\alpha}},{\bar{\beta}}]
with
x\mathbin{\perp\!\!\!\perp}y\equiv\psi(x,y)
.

Proof.

For every natural number
k
, consider the multiteam

\displaystyle M_{k}:=

\displaystyle(\{\mkern-5.0mu\{x\mapsto 0\}\mkern-5.0mu\}\uplus k\{\mkern-5.0mu\{x\mapsto 1\}\mkern-5.0mu\})\times(\{\mkern-5.0mu\{y\mapsto 0\}\mkern-5.0mu\}\uplus k\{\mkern-5.0mu\{y\mapsto 1\}\mkern-5.0mu\})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\mkern-5.0mu\{xy\mapsto 00\}\mkern-5.0mu\}\uplus k\{\mkern-5.0mu\{xy\mapsto 01,xy\mapsto 10\}\mkern-5.0mu\}\uplus k^{2}\{\mkern-5.0mu\{xy\mapsto 11\}\mkern-5.0mu\}.

Notice that
x\mathbin{\perp\!\!\!\perp}y
holds in
M_{k}
for all
k\geq 1
. Further, a simple calculation shows that

x\mathbin{\perp\!\!\!\perp}y
holds in
M_{k}\uplus M_{\ell}
if, and only if,
k=\ell
.
Towards a contradiction we assume that there is a formula
\psi(x,y)\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[{\bar{\alpha}},{\bar{\beta}}]
equivalent to
x\mathbin{\perp\!\!\!\perp}y
.

Let
\mathcal{T}(\psi)
be the syntax tree of
\psi
and choose, for every
k
, an annotation
F_{k}
of
\mathcal{T}(\psi)
,
which associates with every subformula
\varphi\in\mathcal{T}(\psi)
a multiteam
M_{k}(\varphi)
over
\{0,1\}
, and which
witnesses that
M_{k}
satisfies
\psi
. Further, let
T(F_{k})
be the restriction of
F_{k}
to
the supporting teams, mapping
\varphi
to
T(M_{k}(\varphi))
.
Although there are possibly infinitely many different
annotations
F_{k}
, there are only finitely many different restrictions
T(F_{k})
. Hence there exist
k,\ell
with

k\neq\ell
and
T(F_{k})=T(F_{\ell})
.
The union
F_{k}\uplus F_{\ell}
is therefore a structurally valid candidate for witnessing that
the multiteam
M_{k}\uplus M_{\ell}
satisfies
\psi
.
It remains to show that each atomic subformula
\varphi
in
\psi
is satisfied by the the multiteam
M_{k}(\varphi)\uplus M_{\ell}(\varphi)
.
For union closed atoms
\varphi=\beta_{i}
this is clear because
\varphi
holds in
M_{k}(\varphi)
and in
M_{\ell}(\varphi)
.
For atoms
\varphi=\alpha_{j}
and for first-order literals
\varphi
we use the fact that
the underlying team of
M_{k}(\varphi)\uplus M_{\ell}(\varphi)
is the same as the one for
M_{k}(\varphi)
,
and since, by Proposition
2
,
\varphi
only depends on the team, we have that
the multiteam
M_{k}(\varphi)\uplus M_{\ell}(\varphi)
satisfies
\varphi
.
Putting everything together we have that
M_{k}\uplus M_{\ell}
satisfies
\psi(x,y)
but not
x\mathbin{\perp\!\!\!\perp}y
, hence
\psi(x,y)\not\equiv x\mathbin{\perp\!\!\!\perp}y
.
∎

Corollary 6.

{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}},{}\mathbin{\operatorname{|}}{}]\precneqq{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}(\mathbin{\perp\!\!\!\perp})
.

An open problem:

In team semantics, and in fact also in probabilistic team semantics
(
DurandHanKonMeiVir18a
)
,
independence logic is equivalent to conditional independence logic, but the
proofs do not translate to the multiteam setting and
the precise relationship between independence and conditional independence under multiteam semantics remains open.

6.
Back & Forth between Team and Multiteam Semantics

We next discuss the relationship between team semantics and multiteam semantics.
As already pointed out, any team semantical atom
\alpha\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{T}}}
can be understood naturally as a multiteam atom
such that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{(X,n)}\alpha
if, and only if,
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{X}\alpha
.
Conversely, we would like to associate with every multiteam atom
\beta
a team semantical atom
\beta^{\mathsf{T}}
.
We stipulate that
\beta^{\mathsf{T}}
should be the strongest team semantical atom that is implied by
\beta
.
This means, on one side that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{(X,n)}\beta
implies that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{X}\beta^{\mathsf{T}}
,
and on the other side that
\beta^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbin{\vDash}\gamma
for any other atom
\gamma
satisfying
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{(X,n)}\beta
implies
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{X}\gamma
.
These rules imply the following semantics for
\beta^{\mathsf{T}}
.

1.

Given a multiteam atom
\beta
, let
\beta^{\mathsf{T}}
be the team semantical atom with

\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{X}\beta^{\mathsf{T}}
if, and only if,
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{(X,m)}\beta
for some
m\colon X\to\mathbb{N}_{>0}
.

2.

It is easily verified that
({\bar{x}}\mathbin{\perp\!\!\!\perp}{\bar{y}})^{\mathsf{T}}\equiv{\bar{x}}\perp{\bar{y}}
and

({\bar{x}}\sphericalangle_{\leq\frac{1}{2}}y)^{\mathsf{T}}\equiv{\bar{x}}\Upsilon y
.
The team semantical version of a multiteam inclusion atom
{\bar{x}}\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{\bar{y}}
is less obvious.
We have that
({\bar{x}}\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{\bar{y}})^{\mathsf{T}}
is neither
{\bar{x}}\subseteq{\bar{y}}
, nor
{\bar{x}}\Join{\bar{y}}
.
Indeed, the team
\{xy\mapsto 00,xy\mapsto 01,xy\mapsto 11\}
satisfies both
x\subseteq y
and
x\Join y
, but no extension to a multiteam satisfies
x\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}y
.
To define such an atom we introduce an auxiliary concept:
Let
X
be a team and
{\bar{x}},{\bar{y}}\subseteq\dom(X)
be variable tuples of the same length.
The graph
G_{X}^{{\bar{x}},{\bar{y}}}
has vertex set
X({\bar{x}})\cup X({\bar{y}})

and an edge from
{\bar{a}}
to
{\bar{b}}
if there exists an assignment
s\in X
with

s({\bar{x}})={\bar{a}}
and
s({\bar{y}})={\bar{b}}
.
Further, let
\operatorname{cycle}({\bar{x}},{\bar{y}})
be a team semantical atom satisfied by
X
if the graph

G_{X}^{{\bar{x}},{\bar{y}}}
is decomposable into (not necessarily disjoint) cycles.
This means that
G_{X}^{{\bar{x}},{\bar{y}}}=C_{1}\cup\dots\cup C_{k}
for cycles
C_{i}
, where the
union of two graphs
G=(V,E)
and
H=(W,F)
is defined as
G\cup H:=(V\cup W,E\cup F)
( so they may share both vertices and edges).
By item (
5
) of Proposition
3

it then follows that

({\bar{x}}\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{\bar{y}})^{\mathsf{T}}\equiv\operatorname{cycle}({\bar{x}},{\bar{y}})
.

For any collection
\Omega
of multiteam atoms, and the corresponding collection
\Omega^{\mathsf{T}}:=\{\beta^{\mathsf{T}}:\beta\in\Omega\}

we obtain a mapping that associates with every formula
\psi\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\Omega]
a corresponding formula
\psi^{\mathsf{T}}\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{T}}}[\Omega^{\mathsf{T}}]
.
For discussing connections between multiteam and team semantics, we introduce the following notions.

3.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}
and
\varphi\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{T}}}
.
We say that:

•

\psi

depends only on the team
if for all
\mathfrak{A}
and all multiteams
M,N
with
T(M)=T(N)
we have that

\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\varphi
if, and only if,
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{N}\psi
;

•

\varphi
is a
team semantical version
(
tsv
) of
\psi
if for all
\mathfrak{A}
and
X
we have that

\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{X}\varphi
if, and only if,
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\psi
for some multiteam
M
with
T(M)=X
;

•

\varphi
and
\psi
are
companions
if
\varphi
is a
tsv
of
\psi
and
\psi
depends only on the team.

With this terminology, we can restate our second postulate as follows.
For any collection
\Omega
of team semantical atoms, every formula

\varphi\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{T}}}[\Omega]
is a team semantical version of itself, understood
as a formula in
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\Omega]
.

Proposition 4.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}
be a multiteam formula,
\beta
be a multiteam dependency atom and
M
be a multiteam.

(1)

\beta^{\mathsf{T}}
is a team semantical version of
\beta
.

(2)

Whenever
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\psi
then
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{T(M)}\psi^{\mathsf{T}}
.

(3)

If
\psi
depends only on the team then it has a companion
\varphi
.

Proof.

The first two claims follow directly from definition of

\beta^{\mathsf{T}}
by structural induction on the formulae.
Towards (3) we notice that for any multiteam
M
with
X=T(M)
we have that

\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\psi
if, and only if,
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{(X,s\mapsto 1)}\psi
, which, for fixed
\psi
, is an
NP

property of
\mathfrak{A}
and
X
(cf. Corollary
5
).
Hence, by Fagin’s Theorem, there exists a
\operatorname{\Sigma_{1}^{1}}
-sentence
\vartheta
such that

\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{(X,s\mapsto 1)}\psi
if, and only if,
(\mathfrak{A},X)\mathbin{\vDash}\vartheta
.
But since inclusion-exclusion logic
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{T}}}[\subseteq,{}\mathbin{\operatorname{|}}{}]
is equally expressive
as
\operatorname{\Sigma_{1}^{1}}

(
Galliani12
)
, there also exists a formula
\varphi\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{T}}}[\subseteq,{}\mathbin{\operatorname{|}}{}]
such that

(\mathfrak{A},X)\mathbin{\vDash}\vartheta
if, and only if,
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{X}\varphi
.
∎

We further notice that the converse of (2) is not true, so in general

\psi^{\mathsf{T}}
need not be a
tsv
of
\psi
.
To see this consider the formula
\psi=x\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

.
It is easy to see that
\psi^{\mathsf{T}}
is satisfied by the team
X=\{xyuw\mapsto 0101,xyuw\mapsto 1001,xyuw\mapsto 1010\}
.
However, there is no function
n\colon X\to\mathbb{N}_{>0}
so that the multiteam
(X,n)
satisfies
\psi
.

By Proposition
2
, we have that
downwards closed
atoms
\beta\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}

depend only on the team, so
\beta
and
\beta^{\mathsf{T}}
are companions. This extends to
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}
in the following sense.

Proposition 5.

Let
\psi\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\Omega]
for any collection
\Omega
of downwards closed atoms.
Then
\psi
and
\psi^{\mathsf{T}}
are companions.

The following theorem exhibits a stronger relation between downwards closed properties and those that depend only on the team.

Theorem 6.

A formula
\psi\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\operatorname{dep},\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}]
depends only on the team if, and only if, it is downwards closed.

Proof.

The proof of the direction from right to left follows the argument given in
Proposition
2
and uses the fact that all multiteam
atoms are assumed to be closed under scalar multiplication and that the logical
connectives preserve this property (cf. Proposition
1
).

Assume that
\psi
depends only on the team and that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{(X,n)}\psi
.
For arbitrary
s\in X
we show that also
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{(X\setminus\{s\},n)}\psi
.
Let
M_{i}
be the multiteam with base
X
in which all assignments except
s
have multiplicity
i
and
s
occurs just once. By the assumption,
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M_{i}}\psi
for all
i>0
.
For each
i
we fix an annotation
\mathcal{T}_{i}
of the syntax tree of
\psi
witnessing that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M_{i}}\psi
.
Notice that every assignment
s^{\prime}\in X
gives rise to a layer in such an annotation, that is the restriction of
\mathcal{T}_{i}
to just the assignment
s^{\prime}
(in the root) and all of its extensions (universal or existential).
Moreover, note that only a finite number (depending on
X
,
\mathfrak{A}
and
\psi
) of such restrictions exist.
Denote them by
T_{1},\dots,T_{\ell}
.
Hence every
\mathcal{T}_{i}
is decomposable into
a^{i}_{1}\cdot T_{1}\uplus\dots\uplus a^{i}_{\ell}\cdot T_{\ell}
for
a^{i}_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
, where
n\cdot\mathcal{T}
means the annotated syntax tree in which every multiteam is multiplied by the factor
n
.
By the pigeonhole principle, there exist two annotations
\mathcal{T}_{i}
and
\mathcal{T}_{j}
such that regarding their decompositions we have
a^{i}_{1}\leq a^{j}_{1},\dots,a^{i}_{\ell}\leq a^{j}_{\ell}
, where some inequality is strict.
In other words,
\mathcal{T}_{i}(\vartheta)\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\mathcal{T}_{j}(\vartheta)
for all subformulae
\vartheta
of
\psi
.
We can construct now the tree
\mathcal{T}\mathbin{:=}\mathcal{T}_{j}\setminus\mathcal{T}_{i}
, that is defined in the obvious way, by
\mathcal{T}(\gamma)\mathbin{:=}\mathcal{T}_{j}(\gamma)\setminus\mathcal{T}_{i}(\gamma)
. Clearly,
\mathcal{T}
is a structurally valid annotation and thus a possible
witness for
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{(X\setminus\{s\},k)}\psi
.
We claim that, indeed, all atomic formulae are satisfied in
\mathcal{T}
.
This is clear for all downwards closed atoms (and in particular all first-order literals), since they are satisfied by
\mathcal{T}_{j}
already.
For multiteam inclusion atoms
\beta
we conclude from
\mathcal{T}_{i}(\beta)\mathbin{\vDash}\beta
,
\mathcal{T}_{j}(\beta)\mathbin{\vDash}\beta
,
\mathcal{T}_{i}(\beta)\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

and Proposition
3
that also
\mathcal{T}(\beta)\mathbin{\vDash}\beta
holds.
∎

7.
Multiteam semantics and existential second-order logic

A common way for describing the expressive power of a logic with team semantics is to relate it
to a fragment of existential second-order logic with classical Tarski semantics.
The question arises whether there are similar results for logics with multiteam semantics.
Since multiteam semantics involves reasoning about multiplicities of assignments, it must be related
to a framework of classical logic that includes arithmetical operations on natural numbers, but
should, at the same time, keep these separate from the objects in the structures and assignments under consideration.
Such frameworks have been developed, in a very general sense, in
metafinite model theory

(
GraedelGur98
)
.
In general a metafinite structure is a triple
\mathfrak{D}=(\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{N},W)
where
\mathfrak{A}
is a finite structure,
\mathfrak{N}
is a (typically
infinite) numerical structure, possibly equipped with
multiset operations
, and
W
is a set of weight functions

w\colon A^{k}\rightarrow N
mapping tuples from
\mathfrak{A}
to numbers in
\mathfrak{N}
.
Here we use a specific variant of metafinite structure where the numeric part
is the standard model of arithmetic
\mathfrak{N}=(\mathbb{N},0,+,\,\cdot\,,\Sigma)
, together with the summation
operator over arbitrary finite multisets (mapping every finite multiset
M
of natural numbers to the sum
\Sigma(M)
of
its elements).

1.

A
multiteam structure
is a triple
\mathfrak{D}=(\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{N},\{f\})
where
\mathfrak{N}
is as above and
f\colon A^{k}\to\mathbb{N}
is a function, representing a multiteam
M_{f}=(X,m)
with domain
x_{1},\dots,x_{k}
, by
f({\bar{a}})=m(s)
if the assignment
s\colon{\bar{x}}\mapsto{\bar{a}}
is in
X
, and
f({\bar{a}})=0
otherwise.

In metafinite model theory, first-order logic is defined in the standard way, but with the
important stipulation that
variables and quantifiers range only over elements of
\mathfrak{A}
.
Here, we further require that comparisons of terms
t=t^{\prime}
may only be used positively.
Thus, terms and formulae of first-order logic for multiteam structures, denoted
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+,\,\cdot\,]
,
is defined by

t\mathbin{::=}0\mid f{\bar{x}}\mid t+t\mid t\cdot t\mid\Sigma_{{\bar{x}}}\{t:\alpha\}\quad\text{ and }\quad\varphi\mathbin{::=}\alpha\mid t=t\mid\varphi\land\varphi\mid\varphi\lor\varphi\mid\exists x\varphi\mid\forall x\varphi,

where
\alpha
are arbitrary literals in the vocabulary of
\mathfrak{A}
.
The semantics is the usual Tarski semantics. For each multiteam structure

\mathfrak{D}=(\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{N},\{f\})
, and each assignment
s\colon\bar{x}\mapsto\bar{a}
,
it assigns to each term
t(\bar{x})
, a value
t^{\mathfrak{D}}[\bar{a}]\in\mathbb{N}
, and
defines, for each formula
\varphi(\bar{x})
, whether or not
\mathfrak{D}\mathbin{\vDash}\varphi[\bar{a}]
.
Everything is completely standard except perhaps the use of summation as a multiset operation.
Given a term
t(\bar{x},\bar{y})
and a literal
\alpha(\bar{x},\bar{y})
,
the term
\Sigma_{{\bar{x}}}\{t:\alpha\}
binds
\bar{x}

but leaves
\bar{y}
free. Given any assignment
\bar{y}\mapsto\bar{b}
, we obtain the
multiset
M_{t}[\bar{b}]:=\{\mkern-5.0mu\{t^{\mathfrak{D}}[\bar{a},\bar{b}]:\bar{a}\in A^{k},\mathfrak{D}\mathbin{\vDash}\alpha[\bar{a},\bar{b}]\}\mkern-5.0mu\}
.
The value of
\Sigma_{{\bar{x}}}\{t:\varphi\}
for
\mathfrak{D}
and
\bar{b}
is then defined
as
(\Sigma_{\bar{x}}\{t:\alpha\})^{\mathfrak{D}}[\bar{b}]:=\sum M_{t}[\bar{b}]
. To simplify notation
we omit
\alpha
in the trivial case that
\alpha=\mathsf{true}
.
The
Presburger fragment

{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+]

of
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+,\,\cdot\,]
is defined analogously, but forbids multiplication

t\cdot t^{\prime}
of numeric terms.

To illustrate these logics we first show how basic multiteam atoms can be expressed in them.
Let
\mathfrak{D}=(\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{N},\{f\})
be a multiset structure encoding a pair
(\mathfrak{A},M)
,
with
\dom(M)=\{x_{1},\dots,x_{k}\}
. The size of
M

is expressed by the term
|M|:=\Sigma_{\bar{x}}\{f(\bar{x})\}
.
For
i\leq k
, the value of the term
T_{i}(z):=\Sigma_{\bar{x}}\{f(\bar{x}):x_{i}=z\}

on
\mathfrak{D}
, for
z=a
, is
T_{i}^{\mathfrak{D}}[a]=|M_{x_{i}=a}|
, and terms
T_{i_{1}\dots,i_{\ell}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{\ell})

for tuples of values
a_{1},\dots,a_{\ell}
are defined analogously. Then

•

\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}x_{i}\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}x_{j}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \mathfrak{D}\mathbin{\vDash}\forall z\bigl(T_{i}(z)=T_{j}(z)\bigr)
;

•

\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}x_{i}\mathbin{\operatorname{|}}x_{j}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \mathfrak{D}\mathbin{\vDash}\forall\bar{x}\forall\bar{y}\big(x_{i}=y_{j}\rightarrow(f\bar{x}=0\lor f\bar{y}=0)\bigr)
;

•

\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}x_{i}\mathbin{\perp\!\!\!\perp}x_{j}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \mathfrak{D}\mathbin{\vDash}\forall y\forall z\bigl(T_{i}(y)\cdot T_{j}(z)=|M|\cdot T_{ij}(y,z)\bigr)
.

Notice that while inclusion and exclusion atoms are definable in the Presburger fragment of
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}
,
the definition of independence atoms requires multiplication.

To represent multiteam semantics beyond the atomic level, second-order features need to be added.
Different variants for existential second-order logic for metafinite structures (or, equivalently, metafinite spectra) are discussed extensively in
(
GraedelGur98
)
.
It makes a difference, whether existential quantification is
used only over relations ranging over the finite structure
\mathfrak{A}
, or, as needed here, over functions
g\colon A^{k}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
.
Notice however, that the natural closure of
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+,\,\cdot\,]
under existential second-order quantification,
i.e. the set of sentences
\exists g_{1}\dots\exists g_{m}\varphi
, where
\varphi
is in
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+,\,\cdot\,]
, extended to
weight functions
g_{1},\dots,g_{m}
rather than just
f
, is a logic in which one can easily
express all of
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\Omega]
for any collection
\Omega
of first-order definable multiset atoms,
but which is far too powerful, with non-recursive semantics, capturing all r.e. properties of
multiteam structures (see
(
GraedelGur98
, Corollary 4.11)
). Instead we consider a restricted language,
that limits the range of the quantified functions, and uses only the Presburger fragment of
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}
.

2.

We define
{\mathrm{ESO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+]
as the class of formulae of form

\psi(f):=(\exists g_{1}\mathbin{\preceq}b_{1})\dots(\exists g_{m}\mathbin{\preceq}b_{m})\varphi(f,g_{1},\dots,g_{m})

where
\varphi
is in the Presburger fragment
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+]
and where the bounds
b_{i}
are of form
|f|\cdot|A^{\ell_{i}}|
for
\ell_{i}\geq 0
.
The semantics is the obvious one:
\mathfrak{D}=(\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{N},\{f\})\mathbin{\vDash}\psi(f)
if there are functions
g_{1},\dotsc,g_{m}
,
g_{i}\colon A^{r_{i}}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
of appropriate arity
r_{i}
such that
|g_{i}|=\sum_{{\bar{a}}}g_{i}({\bar{a}})\leq|f|\cdot|A|^{\ell_{i}}
and
(\mathfrak{D},g_{1},\dots,g_{m})\mathbin{\vDash}\varphi
.

We first observe that
{\mathrm{ESO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+]
is powerful enough to capture
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}},{}\mathbin{\operatorname{|}}{}]
.
In fact, we prove a stronger result.

Theorem 3.

Let
\Omega
be any collection of multiset atoms that are definable in
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+]
.
For every formula
\varphi(\bar{x})\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\Omega]
there exists a sentence
\psi(f)\in{\mathrm{ESO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+]
, such that for every multiteam structure
\mathfrak{D}=(\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{N},\{f\})
we have that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M_{f}}\varphi
if, and only if,
\mathfrak{D}\mathbin{\vDash}\psi(f)
.

Proof.

If
\varphi({\bar{x}})
is a first-order literal, we translate into
\psi(f):=\forall{\bar{x}}(f{\bar{x}}=0\lor\varphi({\bar{x}}))
.
If
\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\Omega]
are translated into
\psi(f),\psi^{\prime}(f)\in{\mathrm{ESO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+]
, respectively,
then

•

\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\varphi\lor\varphi^{\prime}
if, and only if,
\mathfrak{D}\mathbin{\vDash}(\exists g\mathbin{\preceq}|f|)(\exists h\mathbin{\preceq}|f|)(\forall{\bar{x}}(g{\bar{x}}+h{\bar{x}}=f{\bar{x}})\land\psi(g)\land\psi^{\prime}(h))
,

•

\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\forall y\varphi
if, and only if,
\mathfrak{D}\mathbin{\vDash}(\exists g\mathbin{\preceq}|f|\cdot|A|)(\forall{\bar{x}}\forall y\ g(\bar{x},y)=f(\bar{x})\land\psi(g))
,

•

\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\exists y\varphi
if, and only if,
\mathfrak{D}\mathbin{\vDash}(\exists g\mathbin{\preceq}|f|)(\forall{\bar{x}}(f({\bar{x}})=\Sigma_{y}\{g(\bar{x},y)\})\land\psi(g))
.

∎

It is much more difficult to establish a converse translation.
Since formulae in
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}
are always true for the empty multiteam, we only consider
formulae
\psi(f)\in{\mathrm{ESO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+]
such that
\psi(0)
is a tautology;
notice that for
f=0
, all existential quantifiers
(\exists g_{i}\mathbin{\preceq}b_{i})

trivialise because
b_{i}=0
and all term equalities reduce to
0=0
.
Thus
\psi(0)
reduces to a first-order sentence over
\mathfrak{A}
.
If
\psi(0)
is not a tautology, then we cannot translate
\psi(f)
into a formula

\varphi\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}
because
f=0
encodes the empty multiteam and
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{\{\mkern-5.0mu\{\}\mkern-5.0mu\}}\varphi

holds for all
\mathfrak{A}
.

We start by transforming every sentence of
{\mathrm{ESO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+]
into a normal form which
simplifies the translation of
{\mathrm{ESO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+]
to
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}},{}\mathbin{\operatorname{|}}{}]
afterwards.
Further, all structures are assumed to contain at least two elements, hence we may
quantify over Boolean variables (for which we use Greek letters
\mu_{i},\chi_{i}
).

A sentence
\psi(f)\in{\mathrm{ESO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+]
is in
normal form
if it has the shape

\psi(f)=(\exists g_{1}\mathbin{\preceq}b_{1})\dots(\exists g_{m}\mathbin{\preceq}b_{m})\forall{\bar{x}}\bigvee_{i\leq k}\psi_{i}

where each
\psi_{i}
is a conjunction of first-order literals
\alpha(\bar{x})
and

simple term equalities
, that are terms of form
g{\bar{x}}=0
or

\Sigma_{{\bar{y}}}\{g{\bar{x}}{\bar{y}}\}=\Sigma_{{\bar{v}}}\{h{\bar{v}}{\bar{z}}\}
.

4.

Every sentence
\psi(f)\in{\mathrm{ESO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+]
such that
\psi({\bar{0}})
is a tautology
is equivalent to one in normal form.

Proof.

We assume that
f\neq 0
.
By renaming variables we can make sure that no re-quantification occurs.
Towards first-order existential quantification we make use of Skolemisation as follows:
we substitute a subformula
\exists x\psi
by
(\exists f_{x}\mathbin{\preceq}|f|)(\Sigma_{\bar{x}}\{f{\bar{x}}\}=\Sigma_{y}\{f_{x}y\}\land\forall x(f_{x}x=0\lor\psi))
.
The function
f_{x}
must assign at least one value a non-negative weight because
\Sigma_{\bar{x}}\{f{\bar{x}}\}>0
.

Let
t({\bar{x}})=t^{\prime}({\bar{y}})
be a non simple term occurring in
\varphi
, where simple means either
g{\bar{x}}=0
or
\Sigma_{{\bar{y}}}\{g{\bar{x}}{\bar{y}}\}=\Sigma_{{\bar{v}}}\{h{\bar{v}}{\bar{z}}\}
.
We introduce new appropriately bounded function variables
g_{t}
and
g_{t^{\prime}}
(depending on the functions occurring in the terms
t
and
t^{\prime}
and the number of additions) together with the equalities
g_{t}{\bar{x}}=t({\bar{x}})\land g_{t^{\prime}}{\bar{y}}=t^{\prime}({\bar{y}})\land g_{t}{\bar{x}}=g_{t^{\prime}}{\bar{y}}
and replace the original equality by this expression.
Assume
t^{\prime}
is either
\Sigma_{{\bar{x}}}\{t:\alpha\}
or
g{\bar{y}}+h{\bar{z}}
and occurs in a term other than
f{\bar{v}}=t^{\prime}
.
Introduce the function
g_{t^{\prime}}
together with the equality
g_{t^{\prime}}{\bar{y}}{\bar{z}}=t^{\prime}
and replace all other occurrences of
t^{\prime}({\bar{y}}{\bar{z}})
by
g_{t^{\prime}}{\bar{y}}{\bar{z}}
.
For
f^{\prime}{\bar{x}}=g{\bar{y}}+g^{\prime}{\bar{z}}
we again introduce a fresh function variable
h_{g+g^{\prime}}
of arity
2
bounded by
|f|\cdot|A|^{r+r^{\prime}+1}
, where
r
, resp.
r^{\prime}
, refers to the bound of
g
, resp.
g^{\prime}
.
Moreover, we add
\exists abcd(ac\neq bd\land h_{g+g^{\prime}}ac=g{\bar{y}}\land h_{g+g^{\prime}}bd=g^{\prime}{\bar{z}}\land\forall v\forall w((vw\neq ac\lor vw\neq bd)\rightarrow h_{g+g^{\prime}}vw=0))
.
Hence we can exchange
f^{\prime}{\bar{x}}=g{\bar{y}}+g^{\prime}{\bar{z}}
by
f^{\prime}{\bar{x}}=\Sigma_{xy}\{h_{g+g^{\prime}}xy:\mathsf{true}\}
.
The last non simple term that still might occur is
f{\bar{x}}=\Sigma_{{\bar{y}}}\{g{\bar{y}}{\bar{z}}:\alpha\}
.
The same technique is applied in this case, that is a fresh function variable
h
is quantified with the same arity and bound as
g
.
Further the formula
\forall{\bar{y}}\forall{\bar{z}}((\alpha\rightarrow h{\bar{y}}{\bar{z}}=g{\bar{y}}{\bar{z}})\land(\neg\alpha\rightarrow h{\bar{y}}{\bar{z}}=0))
ensures that
h
behaves as intended, therefore
\Sigma_{{\bar{y}}}\{g{\bar{y}}{\bar{z}}:\alpha\}
can be exchanged by
\Sigma_{{\bar{y}}}\{h{\bar{y}}{\bar{z}}\}
.
Lastly, we may of course replace
g{\bar{x}}
by
\Sigma_{{\bar{y}}}\{g{\bar{x}}\}
.

The next task is to bring all second-order quantifiers in front.
It is clear that function quantification distributes over conjunction and disjunction, but universal quantification has to be treated with care.
Consider the case
\forall x(\exists g\mathbin{\preceq}|f|\cdot|A|^{\ell})\psi
.
Analogously to the translation into Skolem normal form in
\operatorname{\Sigma_{1}^{1}}
, we increase the arity of
g
by one (call it
g^{\prime}
) and its bound by a factor of
|A|
.
Then we replace all occurrences of
g{\bar{y}}
by
g^{\prime}{\bar{y}}x
.
At this point a subtle pitfall appears: since the bound on
g^{\prime}
is increased by a factor of
|A|
in order to store the function
g
depending on
x
it might be the case that for some values
a
of
x
,
\Sigma_{\bar{y}}g^{\prime}{\bar{y}}a>|f|\cdot|A|^{\ell}
, which is not equivalent to stating that for each
x
a
g
of the required bound exists.
Hence we must make sure that the size of
g^{\prime}
for a given
x
is no larger than that of
g
.
The resulting formula thus is
(\exists g^{\prime}\mathbin{\preceq}|f|\cdot|A|^{\ell+1})(\exists\hat{g}\mathbin{\preceq}|f|\cdot|A|^{\ell})\forall x\,(\Sigma_{{\bar{y}}}\{g^{\prime}{\bar{y}}x\}=\Sigma_{z}\{\hat{g}z\}\land\psi[g\mapsto g^{\prime}])
.
Of course, since all variables have unique names we can move all universal quantifiers in front of the (otherwise) quantifier free formula
\psi
to obtain
(\exists g_{1}\mathbin{\preceq}b_{1})\dots(\exists g_{k}\mathbin{\preceq}b_{k})\forall{\bar{x}}\psi
and then rewrite
\psi
to be in disjunctive normal form.

Finally, we may exchange a bound
|g_{i}|\cdot|A|^{r}
by
|f|\cdot|A|^{\ell}
if
g_{i}
is bounded by
|f|\cdot|A|^{\ell-r}
and by iterating this procedure only the required bounds remain.
∎

Theorem 5.

For every sentence
\psi(f)\in{\mathrm{ESO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+]
such that
\psi(0)\equiv\mathsf{true}
, there exists a formula

\varphi\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}},{}\mathbin{\operatorname{|}}{}]
such that, for every multiteam structure
\mathfrak{D}=(\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{N},\{f\})
where
f
encodes a non-empty multiteam
M
,
we have that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\varphi
if, and only if,
\mathfrak{D}\mathbin{\vDash}\psi(f)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4
we may assume that
\psi
is equivalent to

(\exists g_{1}\mathbin{\preceq}b_{1})\dots(\exists g_{m}\mathbin{\preceq}b_{m})\forall{\bar{x}}\bigvee_{i\leq k}\psi_{i}
,
where each
\psi_{i}
is a conjunction of first-order literals and simple term equalities.
For a tuple of variables
{\bar{z}}=z_{1},\dots,z_{m}
and
r\leq m
we use the notation

{\bar{z}}=({\bar{z}}^{\leq r},{\bar{z}}^{>r})
where
{\bar{z}}^{\leq r}=z_{1},\dots,z_{r}
and
{\bar{z}}^{>r}=z_{r+1},\dots,z_{m}
.
To simplify notation we denote
f
by
g_{0}
. Let
b_{i}=|f|\cdot|A|^{\ell_{i}}
,
and let
\ell=\max(\ell_{1},\dots,\ell_{k})
.
The desired formula has the form

\varphi({\bar{y}}_{0})\mathbin{:=}\forall z_{1}\dots\forall z_{\ell}\exists\mu_{0}(\exists{\bar{y}}_{1}\exists\mu_{1})\dots(\exists{\bar{y}}_{m}\exists\mu_{m})\forall{\bar{x}}\;(\vartheta\land\psi^{\#})

where the
\mu_{i}
are Boolean variables and
{\bar{y}}_{i}
is a tuple whose length matches the arity of
g_{i}
.
A multiteam
M
over the variables
{\bar{y}},{\bar{\mu}}
encodes the functions
g_{0}
to
g_{m}

by setting
g_{i}^{M}({\bar{a}})\mathbin{:=}|\{\mkern-5.0mu\{s\in M:s(\mu_{i})=\mathsf{true}\text{ and }s({\bar{y}}_{i})={\bar{a}}\}\mkern-5.0mu\}|
.
The subformula
\vartheta
controls the behaviour of the Boolean variables
\mu_{i}

that indicate which values for
{\bar{y}}_{i}
are used for representing a function value of
g_{i}
.
Notice that the multiteam
M[{\bar{z}}\mapsto A^{\ell}]
has size
|f|\cdot|A|^{\ell}
.
Since this bound may be larger than the bound of
g_{i}
we impose in
\vartheta
that
only assignments satisfying
{\bar{z}}^{>\ell_{i}}=0
can encode values of
g_{i}
, by

\vartheta\mathbin{:=}\bigwedge\nolimits_{i\leq m}\bigl(\bigl({\bar{z}}^{>\ell_{i}}=\bar{0}\rightarrow\operatorname{dep}({\bar{z}}^{\leq\ell_{i}},\mu_{i})\bigr)\land(\mu_{i}\rightarrow{\bar{z}}^{>\ell_{i}}=\bar{0})\bigr).

Let us describe
\psi^{\#}
.
By assumption the quantifier-free part of
\psi
is a disjunction over formulae

\psi_{i}
each of which is a conjunction of literals and simple term equalities.
Thus, we have to split all values for the variables
{\bar{x}}
such that each one
satisfies (at least one) formula
\psi_{i}
.
To simulate this, we quantify over Boolean variables
\chi_{i}
with the indented
semantics that
\chi_{i}
holds in an assignment
s
if
s({\bar{x}})
is distributed to
\psi_{i}
.
Recall that
\alpha\rightarrow\beta
is a shorthand for
\neg\alpha\lor(\alpha\land\beta)
.
Formally,

\psi^{\#}\mathbin{:=}\exists\chi_{1}\dots\exists\chi_{k}(\bigwedge_{i\leq k}\operatorname{dep}({\bar{x}},\chi_{i})\land\bigvee_{i\leq k}\chi_{i}\land\bigwedge_{i\leq k}(\chi_{i}\rightarrow\psi^{\#}_{i})),

where
\psi_{i}^{\#}
is the conjunction of the literals in
\psi_{i}
(without changes) and appropriate translations of its term equations:

•

Equations of form
g_{i}{\bar{u}}=0
are translated into
\mu_{i}\rightarrow{\bar{u}}\neq{\bar{y}}_{i}
.

•

An equation
e({\bar{x}})
of form
\Sigma_{{\bar{v}}}\{g_{i}{\bar{u}}{\bar{v}}\}=\Sigma_{{\bar{w}}}\{g_{j}{\bar{u}}^{\prime}{\bar{w}}\}
where
{\bar{u}},{\bar{u}}^{\prime}
are tuples of length
r
and
s
, respectively, of (not necessarily distinct) variables from
{\bar{x}}
is translated into

\exists\nu\exists\lambda\bigl((\nu\leftrightarrow(\mu_{i}\land{\bar{u}}={\bar{y}}_{i}^{\leq r}))\land(\lambda\leftrightarrow(\mu_{j}\land{\bar{u}}^{\prime}={\bar{y}}_{j}^{\leq s}))\land{\bar{x}}\nu\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

Notice that the variables

, we indeed

\displaystyle\psi(f)

\displaystyle=(\exists g_{1}\mathbin{\preceq}b_{1})\dots(\exists g_{m}\mathbin{\preceq}b_{m})\forall{\bar{x}}\bigvee_{i\leq k}\psi_{i}

\displaystyle\varphi({\bar{y}}_{0})

\displaystyle=\forall z_{1}\dots\forall z_{\ell}\exists\mu_{0}(\exists{\bar{y}}_{1}\exists\mu_{1})\dots(\exists{\bar{y}}_{m}\exists\mu_{m})\forall{\bar{x}}\;(\vartheta\land\psi^{\#}),

\displaystyle\vartheta

\displaystyle=\bigwedge_{i\leq k}\bigl(\bigl({\bar{z}}^{>\ell_{i}}=\bar{0}\rightarrow\operatorname{dep}({\bar{z}}^{\leq\ell_{i}},\mu_{i})\bigr)\land\bigl(\mu_{i}\rightarrow{\bar{z}}^{>\ell_{i}}=\bar{0}\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle\psi^{\#}

\displaystyle=\exists\chi_{1}\dots\exists\chi_{k}(\bigwedge_{i\leq k}\operatorname{dep}({\bar{x}},\chi_{i})\land\bigvee_{i\leq k}\chi_{i}\land\bigwedge_{i\leq k}(\chi_{i}\rightarrow\psi^{\#}_{i})).

Assume that
\mathfrak{D}\mathbin{\vDash}\psi(f)
that is
(\mathfrak{D},g_{1},\dotsc,g_{m})\mathbin{\vDash}\forall{\bar{x}}\bigvee_{i\leq k}\psi_{i}

.

to

. We claim that

.

.
Hence, on any such restriction, the choice function

and

to

for an equal number of assignments.
By the stipulation on these values these numbers correspond to
\Sigma_{{\bar{v}}}\{g_{k}{\bar{u}}{\bar{v}}\}
and
