, respectively.
Therefore
(\mathfrak{D},g_{1},\dots,g_{m})\mathbin{\vDash}e({\bar{a}})
.
∎

By Theorem
3
and
5
, we have a kind of equivalence between the multiteam logic
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\,\mathbin{\operatorname{|}}\,,\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}]
and the existential second-order logic
{\mathrm{ESO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+]
.
Since we know that independence cannot be expressed in
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}},{}\mathbin{\operatorname{|}}{}]
, we infer that it also cannot be defined in
{\mathrm{ESO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+]
.
As we have seen, there is a simple translation of independence atoms into the stronger
variant of
{\mathrm{ESO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}
that uses multiplication. However, this stronger logic cannot be translated back
into a logic with multiteam semantics. Indeed, with multiplication at hand it is not difficult to
write a formula stating that
|f|=1
. But this implies that the logic is not closed under
multiplication of (functions that represent) multiteams.
To come up with an extension of
{\mathrm{ESO}^{\mathsf{mts}}}[+]
that would capture
{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\mathbin{\perp\!\!\!\perp}]
we would have to
add a controlled form of multiplication that avoids such problems. It is not difficult to define
an operator that is essentially a direct translation of independence into the setting of
multiteam structures, but it is unclear at this point whether this is possible with a
natural
algebraic operator.

8.
Multiteams with real weights

We now turn to a different notion of multiteams where the assignments do not come
with a multiplicity in
\mathbb{N}
, but with a
weight
. Although our weight functions need not be probability
distributions and can take arbitrary values in
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
,
this is related to the notion of a probabilistic team as in
(
DurandHanKonMeiVir18a
;
HannulaHirKonKulVir19
)
,
as long as we only consider dependencies that are invariant under
multiplication. This has also been noted in
(
HannulaHirKonKulVir19
)
.

1.

A
real valued multiteam
is a pair
(X,r)
where
X
is a team and
r\colon X\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.

Notions such as
|M|,M\uplus N,M\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}N
and so on are defined as for natural multiteams.
We view a splitting
(X,r)=R_{1}\uplus R_{2}
as the action of a function

\mathcal{S}\colon X\to[0,1]
, with
R_{1}=\mathcal{S}\cdot(X,r)\mathbin{:=}(\{s\in X:\mathcal{S}(s)>0\},s\mapsto r(s)\cdot\mathcal{S}(s))
and
R_{2}=(1-\mathcal{S})\cdot(X,r)
.
Regarding multiteam extensions we put
M[x\mapsto A]=(X[x\mapsto A],r^{\prime}\colon s[x\mapsto a]\mapsto r(s))

and
M[x\mapsto F]=(\{s[x\mapsto a]:s\in X,F(s)(a)>0\},r^{\prime})
with

r^{\prime}\colon s[x\mapsto a]\mapsto r(s)\cdot F(s)(a)
, where
F\colon X\to[0,1]^{A}

is a choice function, that is for all
s\in X
,
\sum_{a\in A}F(s)(a)=1
holds.

The semantics of logical connectives for real valued multiteams is analogous to the one given in Definition
1
.
It only differs slightly for disjunction and existential quantification:

•

\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\varphi_{1}\lor\varphi_{2}\mathbin{:\!\!\iff}\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{\mathcal{S}\cdot M}\varphi_{1}
and
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{(1-\mathcal{S})\cdot M}\varphi_{2}
for some
\mathcal{S}\colon X\mapsto[0,1]
;

•

\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\exists x\varphi\mathbin{:\!\!\iff}\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M[x\mapsto F]}\varphi
for some choice function
F\colon X\to[0,1]^{A}
.

Next we define a notion of approximation for multiteams and the concepts of
topologically open and closed sets of multiteams and formulae.
The intention is that an open formula
\varphi
has the property that if
M
satisfies

\varphi
and
N
is sufficiently close to
M
then also
N
should satisfy
\varphi
.
A topologically closed formula
\psi
will have the property that whenever all
elements of a sequence of multiteams satisfy
\psi
, then also their limit does.

2.

A multiteam
N=(Y,t)
approximates
M=(X,r)
with error
\mathsf{e}_{M}(N)

if
Y\subseteq X
and
\mathsf{e}_{M}(N)=\sum_{s\in X}|r(s)-t(s)|
.
If
Y\nsubseteq X
, we put
\mathsf{e}_{M}(N)=\infty
.

Notice that we only approximate multiteams “from below”, i.e., whenever
\mathsf{e}_{(X,r)}(Y,t)<\infty
then
Y\subseteq X
.
The reason for this choice is that first-order formulae are preserved by subteams but not by superteams.
Hence, if a multiteam
N
has more assignments (in its support) than
M
then the truth of
\varphi
in
M

gives us no indication whether
\varphi
holds in
N
.
The same holds for the opposite notion.
Let
(M_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of multiteams
M_{i}=(X,r_{i})
.
If for every
s\in X
the sequence
(r_{i}(s))_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
converges we define
\lim\limits_{i\to\infty}M_{i}\mathbin{:=}(Y,r)
, where
r(s)\mathbin{:=}\lim\limits_{i\to\infty}r_{i}(s)
and
Y=\{s\in X:r(s)>0\}
.
We call
(M_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
convergent if its limit exists.

3.

A set
\mathcal{M}
of multiteams is called

•

open
if for every
M\in\mathcal{M}
there is some
\varepsilon>0
such that
\mathsf{e}_{M}(N)<\varepsilon
implies
N\in\mathcal{M}
, and

•

closed
if for every convergent sequence
(M_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
of multiteams
M_{i}\in\mathcal{M}
also
\lim\limits_{i\to\infty}M_{i}\in\mathcal{M}
.

We call a formula
\psi

topologically open
or
closed
if for every structure
\mathfrak{A}
the set

\psi^{\mathfrak{A}}=\{M:M\text{ is a multiteam over }A\text{ with }\free(M)=\free(\psi)\text{ and }\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\psi\}
is open, resp. closed.
We write
\overline{\psi^{\mathfrak{A}}}
for the analogous set with
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\nvDash}_{M}\psi
.
As usual, a formula is called
clopen
, if it is both open and closed.
Open and closed sets are related in the usual way, if we tighten the definitions to allow only multiteams of the same support (see the discussion above).

4.

Let
\mathfrak{A}
be a structure,
\psi
be a formula and
X
a team.
Then
\psi^{\mathfrak{A}}{\upharpoonright}_{X}=\{M\in\psi^{\mathfrak{A}}:T(M)=X\}
is open if
\overline{\psi^{\mathfrak{A}}}{\upharpoonright}_{X}=\{M\in\overline{\psi^{\mathfrak{A}}}:T(M)=X\}
is closed and vise versa.

Proof.

Let
\psi^{\mathfrak{A}}{\upharpoonright}_{X}
be open and
(M_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
a convergent sequence of multiteams in
\overline{\psi^{\mathfrak{A}}}{\upharpoonright}_{X}
.
For the sake of the argument assume
M\mathbin{:=}\lim\nolimits_{i\to\infty}M_{i}\notin\overline{\psi^{\mathfrak{A}}}{\upharpoonright}_{X}
.
Then
M\in\psi^{\mathfrak{A}}{\upharpoonright}_{X}
, thus there is some
\varepsilon>0
such that all multiteams with approximation error smaller than
\varepsilon
to
M
also satisfy
\psi
and hence belong to
\psi^{\mathfrak{A}}{\upharpoonright}_{X}
.
But this means that
\lim\nolimits_{i\to\infty}M_{i}\neq M
.

On the other hand, if
\psi^{\mathfrak{A}}{\upharpoonright}_{X}
is closed, assume that
M\in\overline{\psi^{\mathfrak{A}}}{\upharpoonright}_{X}
but for all
\varepsilon>0
there is some
N
with
\mathsf{e}_{M}(N)<\varepsilon
and
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{N}\psi
.
Hence there is a sequence of multiteams
(N_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
approaching
M
satisfying for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{N_{i}}\psi
.
Therefore
\lim\nolimits_{i\to\infty}N_{i}=M\in\psi^{\mathfrak{A}}{\upharpoonright}_{X}
.
∎

The logical connectives preserve topologically open and closed formulae.

5.

Let
\varphi
and
\psi
be topologically open, resp. closed, formulae.
Then
\varphi\land\psi
,
\varphi\lor\psi
and
\forall x\varphi
and
\exists x\varphi
are
topologically open, resp. closed, as well.

Proof.

The proof is carried out by structural induction on the formulae.
For simplicity we assume that no variable is requantified and that the structure

\mathfrak{A}
is fixed.
First, we proof that topologically open formulae are preserved.
Let
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\varphi_{i}
and
\varepsilon^{M}_{\varphi_{i}}>0
depend on
\mathfrak{A}
,
M
and

\varphi_{i}
such that all multiteams
N
that approximate
M
up to an error smaller
than
\varepsilon^{M}_{\varphi_{i}}
also satisfy
\varphi_{i}
under
\mathfrak{A}
.

For conjunction and disjunction we put
\varepsilon^{M}_{\varphi_{1}\circ\varphi_{2}}\mathbin{:=}\min(\varepsilon^{M}_{\varphi_{1}},\varepsilon^{M}_{\varphi_{2}})
.
By induction hypothesis it is clear that this bound suffices for the conjunction.
If
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\varphi_{1}\lor\varphi_{2}
then there is a splitting
\mathcal{S}\colon X\to[0,1]

for
M
s.t.
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M_{i}}\varphi_{i}
with
M_{i}=\mathcal{S}\cdot M
(resp.
1-\mathcal{S}
).
Let
N
be a multiteam with
\mathsf{e}_{M}(N)<\varepsilon^{M}_{\varphi_{1}\lor\varphi_{2}}
.
We claim that
\mathcal{S}
is a splitting for
N
such that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{N_{i}}\varphi_{i}

for
N_{1}=\mathcal{S}\cdot N
and
N_{2}=(1-\mathcal{S})\cdot N
.
Indeed, notice that
\mathsf{e}_{\mathcal{S}\cdot M}(\mathcal{S}\cdot N)\leq\mathsf{e}_{M}(N)
, hence

\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{N_{i}}\varphi_{i}
for
i=1,2
.

Assume
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\forall x\varphi
.
Thus, for
M^{\prime}=M[x\mapsto A]
we have
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M^{\prime}}\varphi
and hence by induction
hypothesis a bound
\varepsilon_{\varphi}^{M^{\prime}}>0
with the required property exists.
Put
\varepsilon_{\forall x\varphi}^{M}\mathbin{:=}\varepsilon_{\varphi}^{M^{\prime}}
and let
N
be a multiteam
in the
\varepsilon_{\varphi}^{M^{\prime}}
environment of
M
, that is
\mathsf{e}_{M}(N)<\varepsilon_{\varphi}^{M^{\prime}}
.
Further, since for
N^{\prime}=N[x\mapsto A]
the errors of
N
to
M
and
N^{\prime}
to
M^{\prime}

are identical, that is
\mathsf{e}_{M^{\prime}}(N^{\prime})=\mathsf{e}_{M}(N)
, and by
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{N^{\prime}}\varphi
,
which follows from
\varphi
being topologically open, we can conclude that also

\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{N}\forall\varphi
as claimed.

Finally, assume
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\exists x\varphi
.
That means a choice function
F
exists such that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M^{\prime}}\varphi
, where

M^{\prime}=M[x\mapsto F]
.
As in the previous case let
\varepsilon_{\exists x\varphi}^{M}\mathbin{:=}\varepsilon_{\varphi}^{M^{\prime}}
and

N
be such that
\mathsf{e}_{M}(N)<\varepsilon_{\exists x\varphi}^{M}
.
For the restriction
F{\upharpoonright}_{N}
of
F
to
N
we obtain
\mathsf{e}_{M}(N)=\mathsf{e}_{M^{\prime}}(N[x\mapsto F{\upharpoonright}_{N}])

which, by induction hypothesis, implies
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{N[x\mapsto F{\upharpoonright}_{N}]}\varphi
.

Before we turn our attention to topologically closed formulae and show that the
logical connectives preserve also this property we recall the Bolzano-Weierstraß Theorem:
Every bounded sequence in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
has a convergent subsequence.

Let
\psi
be a topologically closed formula,
(M_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be a convergent
multiteam sequence over the support
X
such that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M_{i}}\psi
holds
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
and
M=\lim\limits_{i\to\infty}M_{i}
.

•

Regarding conjunction, the property follows directly from induction hypothesis.

•

Consider
\psi=\psi_{1}\lor\psi_{2}
.
For every
i\in\mathbb{N}
let
\mathcal{S}_{i}\colon X\to[0,1]
be a splitting function
witnessing
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M_{i}}\psi
.
By the Bolzano-Weierstraß Theorem there is a convergent subsequence

(\mathcal{S}_{n_{i}})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
of
(\mathcal{S}_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
.
Hence we may apply the induction hypothesis on
(\mathcal{S}_{n_{i}}\cdot M_{n_{i}})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}

and
((1-\mathcal{S}_{n_{i}})\cdot M_{n_{i}})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
to conclude that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\psi_{1}\lor\psi_{2}
.

•

For a universally quantified formula
\forall x\varphi
the claimed property
follows directly from an application of the induction hypothesis.

•

Let
\exists x\varphi
be given where
\varphi
is a topologically closed formula.
Thus
(F_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
exists such that for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
the structure
\mathfrak{A}

satisfies
\varphi
under
M_{i}[x\mapsto F_{i}]
, in symbols
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M_{i}[x\mapsto F_{i}]}\varphi
.
Since
A
is finite and
F\colon X\to[0,1]^{A}
we may apply the
Bolzano-Weierstraß Theorem on the sequence
(F_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
and obtain a subsequence

(F_{n_{i}})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
s.t.
\lim\limits_{i\to\infty}(F_{n_{i}})=\tilde{F}\colon Y\to[0,1]^{A}
exists.
The induction hypothesis is now applicable to the sequence
(M_{n_{i}}[x\mapsto F_{n_{i}}])_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
.

∎

6.

For all (pairwise distinct) variable tuples
{\bar{x}},{\bar{y}},{\bar{z}}
the following atoms are

open but not closed::

{\bar{x}}\sphericalangle_{<q}{\bar{y}}
and
{\bar{x}}\sphericalangle_{>q}{\bar{y}}
for
q\in(0,1)
,

closed but not open::

{\bar{x}}\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

{}{}}}{
{}{}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{\bar{y}}
,
{\bar{x}}\mathbin{\perp\!\!\!\perp}_{{\bar{z}}}{\bar{y}}
and
{\bar{x}}\sphericalangle_{\leq q}{\bar{y}}
and
{\bar{x}}\sphericalangle_{\geq q}{\bar{y}}
for
q\in(0,1)
,

clopen::

\operatorname{dep}({\bar{x}},y),{\bar{x}}\mathbin{\operatorname{|}}{\bar{y}}
and all first-order literals.

Proof.

The simple case are the downwards closed atoms
\delta
(which includes first-order
formulae and hence literals).
It suffices to notice that if
\mathsf{e}_{M}(N)
is finite then
T(N)\subseteq T(M)

which implies that downwards closed atoms are topologically open.
Furthermore, if all multiteams
M_{i}
of a convergent sequence
(M_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}

satisfy
\delta
, then the for the limit
M=\lim\nolimits_{i\to\infty}M_{i}

holds
T(M)\subseteq T(M_{i})
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
which again implies the
claim that
\delta
is topologically closed.

For the atoms claimed to be open, but not closed, take
{\bar{x}}\sphericalangle_{<p}{\bar{y}}
as an
example and let
M
be a multiteam satisfying this atom in
\mathfrak{A}
.
Among all tuples
{\bar{a}}\in T(M)({\bar{x}})
and
{\bar{b}}\in T(M)({\bar{y}})
let
{\bar{a}}_{m}

and
{\bar{b}}_{m}
be such that
\pr_{M}({\bar{y}}={\bar{b}}\mid{\bar{x}}={\bar{a}})
is maximal and put

A=|M_{{\bar{x}}{\bar{y}}={\bar{a}}_{m}{\bar{b}}_{m}}|
,
B=|M_{{\bar{x}}={\bar{a}}_{m}}|
and
m=pB-A
(i.e.
\pr_{M}({\bar{y}}={\bar{b}}\mid{\bar{a}}={\bar{x}})=A/B
).
Pick an arbitrary
\varepsilon
satisfying the range condition
0<\varepsilon<\frac{m}{1+p}
.
Let
N
be a real weight multiteam with
\mathsf{e}_{M}(N)<\varepsilon
and
{\bar{a}}\in T(N)({\bar{x}})
and
{\bar{b}}\in T(N)({\bar{y}})
.
Notice that
\pr_{N}({\bar{y}}={\bar{b}}\mid{\bar{x}}={\bar{a}})
is bounded from above by
\frac{A+\varepsilon}{B-\varepsilon}
.
A basic calculation shows that
\pr_{N}({\bar{y}}={\bar{b}}\mid{\bar{x}}={\bar{a}})<p
:
Indeed,
\frac{A+\varepsilon}{B-\varepsilon}<p\iff\varepsilon<pB-A-p\varepsilon=m-p\varepsilon\iff 1<\frac{m}{\varepsilon}-p\iff\varepsilon<\frac{m}{1+p}
, which by definition of
\varepsilon
holds.

Regarding the closed atoms take
{\bar{x}}\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{\bar{y}}
as an example.
A multiteam
(X_{i},r_{i})
satisfies this atom if for all appropriate
{\bar{a}}
the
equation
\sum_{s\in X_{i},s({\bar{x}})={\bar{a}}}r_{i}(s)=\sum_{s\in X_{i},s({\bar{y}})={\bar{b}}}r_{i}(s)
holds.
Further, consider any convergent sequence of multiteams
(M_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
that each satisfy
{\bar{x}}\mathbin{\hbox to11.11pt{\vbox to8.12pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.41667pt\lower-4.05971pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-6.41667pt}{-1.9618pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{\bar{y}}
.
It follows directly that the equation above must be satisfied by the limit of this sequence as well (as we can take the limit on both sides of the equation).
The claim holds for the other atoms as well, since independence is similarly defined over an equation and the forking atoms are satisfied also for equality.
∎

It follows that logics based on topologically open, resp. closed, atoms are expressively separable.
Towards the question concerning which properties of the supporting team can be defined in a logic with real multiteam semantics
we explore the properties of clopen formulae.
Let
\Omega
be a set of (multiplicity invariant) atomic dependency notions.

Proposition 7.

For any
\psi\in\mathrm{FO}[\Omega]
the following are equivalent.

(1)

\psi
is clopen.

(2)

For all structures
\mathfrak{A}
and all
M,N
with
\mathsf{e}_{M}(N)<\infty
we have that

\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\psi
implies
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{N}\psi
.

(3)

\psi
is downwards closed.

Proof.

The implications “
2

\implies

1
” and “
2

\implies

3
” are immediate.
Regarding “
3

\implies

2
” assume
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\psi
,
and let
N
be a multiteam with
\mathsf{e}_{M}(N)<\infty
, that means
T(N)\subseteq T(M)
.
This implies
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{|N|\cdot M}\psi
and by downwards closure of
\psi
also
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{N}\psi
as required.
Towards “
1

\implies

2
” assume that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\psi
.
For a fixed support
X
there is a bijection
\rho
mapping every multiteam with support
Y\subseteq X
to an element of
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{n}
where
n=|X|
.
Indeed, for a fixed ordering of
X=\{s_{1},\dots,s_{n}\}
, every multiteam
(Y,r)
can be written as a tuple
t\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{n}
, where the
i
th entry of
t
is
r(s_{i})
.
We associate the set
\mathcal{T}=\{t\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{n}:\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{\rho^{-1}(t)}\psi\}
with
\mathfrak{A}
,
\psi
and
X
.
Then,
\mathcal{T}
is clopen in the classical sense if, and only if,
\psi^{\mathfrak{A}}{\upharpoonright}_{X}
is clopen.
It is well known that the only clopen sets of a connected topological space are
\varnothing
and the whole space itself.
Because
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{M}\psi
it follows that
\mathcal{T}\neq\varnothing
, whence
\mathcal{T}=\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{n}
.
∎

Corollary 8.

Every topologically closed formula
\psi\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\Omega]
defining a team semantical property, meaning that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{(X,r)}\psi
if, and only if,
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}_{(X,r^{\prime})}\psi
holds for all
\mathfrak{A},X,r
and
r^{\prime}
, is downwards closed.

Proof.

The assumption on
\psi
implies that
\psi
must be topologically open as well, hence by Proposition
7
it follows at once that
\psi
is downwards closed.
∎

This corollary does not hold for open formulae.
Indeed, consider
x\sphericalangle_{<1}y
which expresses that every value of
x
in the multiteam
appears with at least two values for
y
in common assignments.
This property clearly depends only on the team and holds regardless of the involved multiplicities (because they are all greater than 0).
In logics with team semantics this atom is known as
anonymity

x\Upsilon y
, which is union closed but not downwards closed.

As an application of this theory we show that the converse of Proposition
5
holds.
To avoid confusion we write
\mathbin{\vDash}^{\mathsf{T}}
,
\mathbin{\vDash}^{\mathbb{N}}
, and
\mathbin{\vDash}^{\mathbb{R}}
, for team semantic, multiteam semantics under natural and real numbers, respectively.
Let
\Omega
be a collection of topologically closed atoms.

Proposition 9.

A formula
\psi\in{\mathrm{FO}^{\mathsf{M}}}[\Omega]
is downwards closed if, and only if,
\psi^{\mathsf{T}}
and
\psi
are companions.

Proof.

It remains to prove the direction from right to left.
Assume that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}^{\mathbb{N}}_{(X,n)}\psi
.
Then, a fortiori,
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}^{\mathbb{R}}_{(X,n)}\psi
.
Since this holds for arbitrary
n\colon X\to\mathbb{N}_{>0}
we may apply Corollary
8
(from
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}^{\mathbb{N}}_{(X,n)}\psi
for all
n\colon X\to\mathbb{N}_{>0}
follows
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}^{\mathbb{R}}_{(X,r)}\psi
for all
r\colon X\to\mathbb{R}_{>0}
because
\psi
is topologically closed).
Hence for all
Y\subseteq X
,
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}^{\mathbb{R}}_{(Y,t)}\psi
and, in general,
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}^{\mathbb{R}}_{(X,r)}\vartheta
implies that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}^{\mathsf{T}}_{X}\vartheta^{\mathsf{T}}
.
Thus,
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}^{\mathsf{T}}_{Y}\psi^{\mathsf{T}}
and by the fact that
\psi^{\mathsf{T}}
and
\psi
are companions we conclude that
\mathfrak{A}\mathbin{\vDash}^{\mathbb{N}}_{(Y,n)}\psi
.
∎
